. Applying move (B2) or (B3) changes the braid word according to a commutation or braid move, while (B5) changes the braid word by cyclic rotation.

Remark 8.1
.

Technically,
[
CGG+25
, Proposition 4.46]
only treats the case of inductive weaves, as opposed to general double inductive weaves. Nevertheless, the same proof shows that the same result holds for double inductive weaves.∎

As a final comment,
[
CGG+25
, Section 8.3]
describes geometrically the Donaldson-Thomas (DT) transformation on
X(\beta)
as the composition of two isomorphisms, cyclic rotation
\rho
and the map
\ast
. Using
Table

5
, we can describe the pullback of the DT transformation
\ast\circ\rho^{l}
explicitly as a sequence of mutations and rescalings in the context of
[
GLSB23
]
, as follows. To ease notation, if
\bbbeta^{\prime}
is related to
\bbbeta
by a sequence of double braid moves, corresponding to an isomorphism
\phi
of double braid varieties, we will write just
\bbbeta^{\prime}
for
\phi^{*}(\Sigma_{\bbbeta^{\prime}}^{\rm D})
, the pullback of
\Sigma_{\bbbeta^{\prime}}^{\rm D}
to
\mathbb{C}[R(\bbbeta)]
. Then, given a double braid word
\bbbeta=i_{1}\dots i_{l}
with all positive letters, the pullback
(\ast\circ\rho^{l})^{*}
of DT applied to
\Sigma_{\bbbeta}^{\rm D}
agrees with the following sequence of rescalings and mutations

i_{1}\dots i_{l}\xrightarrow{{\rm(B5)}}(-i_{1})i_{2}\dots i_{l}\xrightarrow{{\rm(B1)}}\dots\xrightarrow{{\rm(B1)}}i_{2}\dots i_{l}(-i_{1})\xrightarrow{{\rm(B4)}}i_{2}\dots i_{l}i_{1}^{*}\xrightarrow{{\rm(B5)}}(-i_{2})\dots i_{l}i_{1}^{*}\xrightarrow{{\rm(B1)}}\dots

\dots\xrightarrow{{\rm(B4)}}i_{3}\dots i_{l}i_{1}^{*}i_{2}^{*}\xrightarrow{{\rm(B5)}}\cdots\cdots\xrightarrow{{\rm(B1)}}i_{1}^{*}\dots(-i_{l})\xrightarrow{{\rm(B4)}}i_{1}^{*}\dots i_{l}^{*}.

9.
Appendix C: Richardson varieties and braid varieties

This short appendix discusses the relation between Richardson varieties and braid varieties, with comments on the relation between different cluster algebra structures in Type A. The main contribution is
9.3
, where a precise comparison is established between the weave cluster seeds, the Deodhar cluster seeds, the cluster seeds constructed by B. Leclerc, and the cluster seeds constructed by G. Ingermanson.

For
v,w\in W
, the (open) Richardson variety
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
is defined as the intersection

\mathcal{R}_{v,w}:=(\mathsf{B}_{-}v\mathsf{B}_{+}/\mathsf{B}_{+})\cap(\mathsf{B}_{+}w\mathsf{B}_{+}/\mathsf{B}_{+})\subset G/\mathsf{B}_{+}.

We also have the isomorphic varieties in different flag varieties:

{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}:=(\mathsf{B}_{-}\backslash\mathsf{B}_{-}v\mathsf{B}_{+})\cap(\mathsf{B}_{-}\backslash\mathsf{B}_{-}w\mathsf{B}_{-})\subset\mathsf{B}_{-}\backslash G,

\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}:=(\mathsf{B}_{+}v\mathsf{B}_{-}/\mathsf{B}_{-})\cap(\mathsf{B}_{-}w\mathsf{B}_{-}/\mathsf{B}_{-})\subset G/\mathsf{B}_{-}.

9.1.
Summary on cluster algebras structures for Richardson varieties

B. Leclerc defined a conjectural cluster structure on
{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
in types ADE in
[
Lec16
]
. G. Ingermanson defined an upper cluster structure on
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
in type A in
[
Ing19
]
; this was compared to the Deodhar cluster structure in
[
GLSBS22
, Section 10.2]
and to B. Leclerc’s type A conjectural cluster structure in
[
SSB24
]
. These comparisons respectively established that G. Ingermanson’s and B. Leclerc’s constructions give cluster structures on Richardson varieties. E. Ménard defined a conjectural cluster structure on
{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
in types ADE in
[
Men21
]
, which was shown to be an upper cluster structure in
[
CK22
]
. E. Ménard’s construction was compared to the weave cluster structure in
[
CGG+25
, Section 10]
, where it was also shown to give a cluster structure on Richardson varieties.

In the following sections, we record the precise relationships between these cluster structures on Richardson varieties, and the weave and Deodhar cluster structures on braid varieties. These results are largely proved in
[
CGG+25
,
GLSBS22
,
SSB24
]
; we compile them here for convenience.

9.2.
Identifying Richardson varieties with braid varieties

Comparing cluster structures on
\mathcal{R}_{v,w},{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
and (certain) braid varieties requires a choice of isomorphism between the varieties in question. In this subsection, we introduce a number of maps which we will use to define these isomorphisms.

First, we have that the projection maps
\mathsf{B}_{-}\backslash\mathsf{G}\longleftarrow\mathsf{G}\longrightarrow\mathsf{G}/\mathsf{B}_{-}
,
\mathsf{B}_{-}g\mapsfrom g\mapsto g\mathsf{B}_{-}
, restrict to isomorphisms

(9.1)

{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}\xleftarrow{\delta_{1}}\dot{v}U_{+}\cap U_{+}\dot{v}\cap\mathsf{B}_{-}w\mathsf{B}_{-}\xrightarrow{\delta_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}.

The
right chiral map

\vec{\chi}_{v,w}:{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}
is defined as
\vec{\chi}_{v,w}:=\delta_{2}\circ\delta_{1}^{-1}
. By definition, the inverse isomorphism is the
left chiral map

\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\chi\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\chi\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\chi\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\chi\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{v,w}:\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}\xrightarrow{\sim}{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
.

Second, we have three involutions on
\mathsf{G}
, two anti-automorphisms
g\mapsto g^{T}
and
g\mapsto g^{\iota}
and one automorphism
g\mapsto g^{\theta}=(g^{\iota})^{T}=(g^{T})^{\iota}
. These maps are defined by:

(9.2)

\displaystyle a^{T}=a\quad(a\in H),

\displaystyle x_{i}(t)^{T}=y_{i}(t),\qquad

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)^{T}=x_{i}(t)

(9.3)

\displaystyle a^{\iota}=a^{-1}\quad(a\in H),

\displaystyle x_{i}(t)^{\iota}=x_{i}(t),\qquad

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)^{\iota}=y_{i}(t)

(9.4)

\displaystyle a^{\theta}=a^{-1}\quad(a\in H),

\displaystyle x_{i}(t)^{\theta}=y_{i}(t),\qquad

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)^{\theta}=x_{i}(t).

Note that
\dot{s}_{i}^{\iota}=\dot{s}_{i}
and
\dot{s}_{i}^{T}=\dot{s}_{i}^{\theta}=\dot{s}_{i}^{-1}
and
\mathsf{B}_{-}^{\theta}=\mathsf{B}_{+}
. It follows that
g\mapsto g^{\theta}
descends to an involutive isomorphism

(9.5)

\Theta:G/\mathsf{B}_{+}\xrightarrow{\sim}G/\mathsf{B}_{-},\quad\qquad g\mathsf{B}_{+}\mapsto g^{\theta}\mathsf{B}_{-}

which restricts to an involutive isomorphism
\Theta:\mathcal{R}_{v,w}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}
.

Finally,
[
GL22
, Definition 2.5]
introduced an isomorphism
\vec{\tau}^{\pre}_{v,w}:\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}\to{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
. The
right twist

\vec{\tau}_{v,w}:=\Theta^{-1}\circ\vec{\chi}_{v,w}\circ\vec{\tau}^{\pre}_{v,w}\circ\Theta
is then an automorphism of
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
.

Explicitly, we have

\vec{\tau}^{\pre}_{v,w}\circ\Theta:g\mathsf{B}_{+}\mapsto\mathsf{B}_{-}(v[g^{\iota}\dot{w}]_{L}^{+}),

where
x_{L}^{+}\in U_{+}
is the first component in the image of
x\in\mathsf{B}_{+}\mathsf{B}_{-}
under the canonical isomorphism
\mathsf{B}_{+}\mathsf{B}_{-}\overset{\sim}{\to}U_{+}\times H\times U_{-}
. For instance, in type A this isomorphism is the UDL decomposition.

Remark 9.1
.

As braid varieties are defined using right
\mathsf{B}_{+}
-cosets, our preference is to phrase
all cluster structures on
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
. In what follows, we use the isomorphisms

\Theta:\mathcal{R}_{v,w}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle\chi\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle\chi\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle\chi\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\chi\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{v,w}\circ\Theta^{-1}:\mathcal{R}_{v,w}\xrightarrow{\sim}{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}

to identify
\mathcal{R}_{v,w},\mathcal{R}^{-}_{v,w},{{}^{-}\mathcal{R}}_{v,w}
when necessary, and use their pullbacks to identify the appropriate coordinate rings.∎

9.3.
Comparison between cluster structures in Type A Richardson varieties

Fix
v\leq w\in W
and let
\mathbf{w}
be a reduced expression for
w
. The constructions cited above of B. Leclerc, E. Ménard, and G. Ingermanson
7
7

7

Technically, Ingermanson only gives a seed for the
unipeak
expression for
w
.
have in common that each reduced expression
\mathbf{w}
gives rise to a seed. We denote these seeds by
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}},\Sigma^{\Men}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
, and
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
, respectively. By the convention in
Remark

9.1
, all seeds here are seeds for the Richardson variety
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
: e.g. the functions of
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
differ from those defined by B. Leclerc by the pullback in
Remark

9.1
. The cluster variables of
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
and
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
are indexed by the letters in the complement of the
rightmost
subexpression for
v
in
\mathbf{w}
.

Let
\beta=s_{i_{1}}\dots s_{i_{\ell}}
be a reduced word for
w_{\circ}v
, and let
\gamma=s_{j_{1}}\dots s_{j_{r}}
be the reverse of
\mathbf{w}
. We define an isomorphism

p:\mathcal{R}_{v,w}\longrightarrow X(\beta\gamma)
via

p:g\mathsf{B}_{+}\mapsto(\mathsf{B}_{+}\xrightarrow{s_{i_{1}}}\cdots\xrightarrow{s_{i_{\ell}}}\dot{w_{\circ}}g^{-\iota}\mathsf{B}_{+}\xrightarrow{s_{j_{1}}}\cdots\xrightarrow{s_{j_{r}}}w_{\circ}\mathsf{B}_{+}).

Remark 9.2
.

There are many ways to identify braid varieties and Richardson varieties. This isomorphism
p
agrees with
[
GLSBS22
, Eq. (10.2)]
, up to composition with
\varphi
, and was chosen because it sends the Lusztig-positive part of
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
to the cluster-positive part of
X(\beta\gamma)
. It differs from the isomorphisms of
[
CGG+25
, Section 3.6]
and
[
GLSBS22
, Section 2.7]
.∎

The relation between all the different cluster seeds in Type A, i.e. weave seeds, Deodhar seeds, and the two types of seeds constructed by B. Leclerc and G. Ingermanson, is established in the following result:

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
v,w\in S_{n}
and let
\mathbf{w}
be a reduced expression for
w
. Let
\beta,\gamma
be as above and define the double string
\ddot{\mathbf{s}}=(s_{i_{1}}R,\dots,s_{i_{\ell}}R,s_{j_{1}}R,\dots,s_{j_{r}}R)
. Let
\bbbeta
be the double braid word
\bbbeta:=s_{j_{r}}\cdots s_{j_{1}}s_{i_{\ell}}\cdots s_{i_{1}}
. Then we have the following relation between cluster seeds for the Type A Richardson variety
\mathcal{R}_{v,w}
:

\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}\circ\varphi^{-1}\circ p=\Sigma^{\operatorname{W}}_{\ddot{\mathbf{s}}}\circ p=\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}=\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}\circ\vec{\tau}_{v,w}.

A word is warranted regarding indices in the above equalities. The index set for
\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}
is
J^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}
, the complement of the rightmost subexpression for
w_{\circ}
in
\bbbeta
. All letters of the suffix
s_{i_{\ell}\dots s_{i_{1}}}
, a reduced word for
v^{-1}w_{\circ}
, are in the rightmost subexpression for
w_{\circ}
. The remaining letters of
\bbbeta
in this subexpression form the rightmost subexpression for
v
in
s_{j_{r}}\dots s_{j_{1}}=\mathbf{w}
. Thus there is a natural bijection between
J^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}
and the index sets for
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
and
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
. The corresponding equality in
9.3
above utilizes this bijection.

Proof of
9.3
.

The equality
\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}\circ\varphi^{-1}=\Sigma^{\operatorname{W}}_{\ddot{\mathbf{s}}}
follows from the
Main Theorem
, which yields the first equality. As mentioned above, technically the seed
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
is defined only when
\mathbf{w}
is a unipeak expression, i.e. in the wiring diagram, strands never go up after they begin to go down. Every permutation has a unipeak expression. For
\mathbf{w}
not unipeak, we directly define
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}:=\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}
. For
\mathbf{w}
unipeak, the equality
\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}=\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}\circ\vec{\tau}_{v,w}
of the third and fourth seeds is
[
SSB24
, Theorem B]
and the equality
\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}\circ\varphi^{-1}\circ p=\Sigma^{\Ing}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
of the first and third seeds is
[
GLSBS22
, Prop. 10.5 & Rmk. 10.8]
. These equalities for arbitrary
\mathbf{w}
follow from the fact that commutation and braid moves on
\mathbf{w}
and
\bbbeta
give rise to relabelings and mutations at the same indices in both
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
and
\Sigma^{{\operatorname{D}}}_{\bbbeta}
. See the proof of
[
SSB24
, Proposition 7.1]
for the effect of commutation and braid moves on
\Sigma^{\Lec}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

9.3
does not feature E. Ménard’s seeds
\Sigma^{\Men}_{v,\mathbf{w}}
, but it follows from
[
CGG+25
, Section 10]
that the quiver coincides with the quivers of the seeds in
9.3
.∎
